Counts are substantive places (one row may differ from both witnesses), not tokens. The High/Medium/Low columns above count each substantive place once. Certainty-only changes are recorded in the doubts/decisions apparatus and excluded from these counts. Macro syntax and H editorial matter are excluded. Confidence describes the reading at the place.

## Consequential corrections

- Scan 080, (102), lower-left object: underline and cancelled argument: \Pi(M_{\underline G}\deleted{,\unsure{\Gamma^!_{\underline G,\underline G'}}}) — A detached short horizontal bar lies below the low right foot of the G before the cancellation. The cancelled following expression remains doubtful. The adjacent cancelled argument remains visible as crossed mathematical ink; H omits it from its object. Its obscured superscript and indices remain doubtful.
- Scan 080, (102), upper-right object: \Pi M_{G'} — The G has a low rightward foot but no separate bar below it; the prime is present.
- Scan 080, (102), lower-right object: \Pi M_{G,G'} — Neither G in this lower-right index has the detached bar seen under the upper source indices.
- Scan 080, (103), upper-right object: \Pi M_{G'} — The right target has a G with its ordinary foot and a prime, without a second underlining stroke.
- Scan 080, (103), downward arrow label: \varphi_{G,G'} — The label has a closed upper loop and a long downward stem; its two G indices have no detached underlines.
- Scan 080, (103), lower object: G under M: \Pi(M_G,\deleted{\unsure{\Gamma^!_{\underline G,\underline G'}}}) — The G under M has no separate underline before the comma. The bars visible to its right belong to the cancelled later indices. The crossed argument remains represented locally as a doubtful Gamma expression; H substitutes an ellipsis without the cancellation. Blind audit: the distinct comma after M_G is left of the first hatching stroke and remains live; C incorrectly includes it in the deletion.

The final line contains a drawn right arrow between the two underlined Gs.
- Scan 080, Main paragraph first formula: \varphi_{G,G'} — The two small G indices have no detached underlining strokes.

### Unresolved places

- (102), cancelled argument after M_underlined_G: \Gamma^!_{\underline G,\underline G'}. Dense hatching prevents certification of the full Gamma superscript and indices; C already marked this cancellation doubtful.
- (103), horizontal arrow label: \gamma_{\underline G,\underline G'}. Overdrawing obscures the distinguishing central strokes; retain M1077.
- (103), cancelled lower argument: \Gamma^!_{\underline G,\underline G'}. Gamma and low indices are partly visible, but cancellation crosses the superscript and parts of both letters.
- Cancelled prose beside (103), before pour la suite: mais. A short connected group appears before pour, but the diagonal strike crosses it.
- Cancelled prose beside (103), before lieu de: au. The small group at the right end of the preceding cancelled line is not fully exposed.
- Cancelled prose beside (103), final Gamma expression: \Gamma^!_{\underline G,\underline G'}. Preserve the candidate rather than assert the complete expression has become clear.
- Main line 2, deleted word after de: la. Only portions of the loop and following stroke are exposed beneath the dark crossing-out; C's uncertainty remains.
- Main line 3, original cancelled base argument: \Gamma_{\underline G,\underline G'}. The original argument and superscribed insertion are struck together; several original index strokes are obscured.
- Main line 8, cancelled argument of Pi(M_G): \Gamma^!_{\underline G,\underline G'}. The dense diagonal hatching covers the raised position and parts of the lower letters.
- NB first line, cancelled argument: \Gamma_{\underline G,\underline G'}. The cancellation conceals parts of Gamma and the indices, although some detached index bars remain visible.
- NB after qui précède: incluse. The short connected word before the comma cannot be certified letter by letter; the remainder of the former doubt is readable.
- Last main line, struck word beneath arêtes: flèches. The shorter outline and initial f/l favour flèches, but medial and terminal strokes overlap the horizontal strike. The requirement that every letter be plain is not satisfied, so the alternative remains.
- First display number, last digit: \unsure{102|107}. The small last figure has a rounded upper bend and descending stroke but no secure lower foot. The blind audit therefore leaves 2/7 open; numerical sequence is not used to force102.

### Unresolved places

- Line 2, cancelled word after the first full stop: \deleted{\unsure{Plus}}. P and the tall looped l are plain, but the terminal u/s sequence merges with the heavy horizontal strike. The complete word is therefore not resolved merely because both witnesses suggest Plus.
- M1081, first index of gamma on line 5: \underline G\unsure{{}^*|{}'}. Several compact crossing/looped strokes are visible where a superscript stands. They favour an asterisk over the adjacent prime’s single stroke, but the local thickening prevents a secure reading. The blind reader proposed delta; a distinct delta body remains inseparable from the retraced raised strokes.
- Underlined designation: scope on sur: \unsure{\uline{sur}|sur}. The first underline ends after générisation; the following stroke begins beneath the end of the short sur region before les. Full-word scope is not settled.
- Second paragraph: final s of d’homs: d'hom\unsure{s}. The low terminal stroke after m does not securely distinguish a separate s from its tail on the blind crop.
- Cancelled diagram: attachment of star: \Pi\unsure{M^*_{\underline G,\underline G'}|M_{\underline G^*,\underline G'}}. The clear asterisk is above/right of the first G, but the compressed handwritten placement does not securely distinguish a superscript on M from one on its first graph index.

### Unresolved places

- (105) top-left middle index: \unsure{G',}. Crossing strokes overlap the letter and/or prime; an unqualified recovery is not warranted.
- (105) lower-left final index: \unsure{G'}. Crossing strokes overlap the letter and/or prime; an unqualified recovery is not warranted.
- prose after en tant qu'ext., Pi M_G cancelled index: \unsure{,G'}. Crossing strokes overlap the letter and/or prime; an unqualified recovery is not warranted. The comma-like stroke is included in the same local doubt.
- prose after extension de, Pi M_G cancelled index: \unsure{,G'}. Crossing strokes overlap the letter and/or prime; an unqualified recovery is not warranted. The comma-like stroke is included in the same local doubt.
- (107) upper-left middle index: \unsure{G',}. Crossing strokes overlap the letter and/or prime; an unqualified recovery is not warranted.
- (107) gamma middle index: \unsure{G',}. Crossing strokes overlap the letter and/or prime; an unqualified recovery is not warranted.
- (107) phi final index: \unsure{,G'}. Crossing strokes overlap the letter and/or prime; an unqualified recovery is not warranted. The comma-like stroke is included in the same local doubt.
- (107) lower-object final index: \unsure{,G'}. Crossing strokes overlap the letter and/or prime; an unqualified recovery is not warranted. The comma-like stroke is included in the same local doubt.
- (105) cancelled central object subscript: \underline{\unsure{G'}}. The underline is separate and visible, but a prime cannot be isolated beneath the zigzags.
- (105) right middle cancelled index: \unsure{G''}. One or more of the upper short strokes may be part of cancellation.
- Crossed fragment under (105): \unsure{(\simeq\Pi M)}. Only the opening parenthesis is clear; the rest of the dense cancellation and terminal boundary remain doubtful.
- First prose cancelled word: \unsure{enlevé|oublié}. Thick horizontal strike masks several letters; the visible tall stroke and end accent favor enlevé without settling it.
- On, cancelled first word: \unsure{tire}. The tall opening and i dot favor tire; the stroke overlays the middle and terminal letters.
- (106), cancelled third index: \unsure{,G''|,G'}. Two tiny upper marks appear to the right, but one may be the end of a cancellation stroke.
- (107), downward shaft ending: \unsure{\downarrow|\vert}. A small terminal return and rightward flick suggest a tiny head; the feature is much less distinct than other arrows.
- Two isolated strokes beneath/right of (105): two horizontal TikZ strokes. Their dark ink and coherent horizontal shapes are plain, but the page gives no semantic attachment.
- (105), far-left curved phi label final index: \varphi_{G,\unsure{G''}}. The label meets the curved shaft; the terminal letter/primes cannot all be isolated.
- First prose paragraph, cancelled on a after et: \unsure{on a}. The e and tall t of et are exposed; the small following bodies are crossed and not all recoverable.
- Raised and subsequently cancelled a défini: \unsure{a défini}. The horizontal strike traverses the small letters; the tall ascenders alone do not certify the whole phrase.
- End of extension paragraph, after tordu: \unsure{.}. A dark point at the expected terminal position is contiguous with the black edge; the supplementary right-edge crop does not separate them.

### Unresolved places

- M1085, sign before c’est: \unsure{$\ast$}. The overlapping cross-strokes and long descending stem do not securely identify a simple asterisk or a correction sign.
- Cancelled upper-right expression, final group term: \unsure{\Gamma_G}. Dense oblique cancellation masks the old group symbol and its subscript. The candidate is retained rather than asserting that every letter is plain.
- Cancelled upper-right expression, inner opening and second closing parenthesis: (\Pi\unsure{(}M_G,\unsure{\Gamma_G})\unsure{)}. One enclosing pair can be followed, but cancellation intersects the possible parenthesis immediately after Pi and the possible additional closing parenthesis. The extra delimiter candidates are marked locally.
- Cancelled interlinear de after the first lattice: \inserted{\deleted{d\unsure{e}}}. The d remains readable, while the e body merges with the horizontal strike.
- Old group term in the upper target of (115): \deleted{\unsure{\Gamma_G}}. The old term is heavily hatched. The lowered G-shaped remnants are visible, but the complete old Gamma term is not wholly plain.
- Lower connector in (115): \unsure{\text{ou}|{-}|\to}. Rounded onset plus a flattened trailing return resemble ou in the parallel lower-left cell, but a short dash or arrow is not excluded.
- Cancelled equality after S_A Pi M_G: \unsure{\Gamma|F}. The capital’s middle horizontal strokes overlap the cancellation; its identity is not fully exposed.
- (114), cancelled upper capital before live subscript G: \deleted{\unsure{\Gamma}}_G. The capital is hatched, while the lower G is exposed outside that hatching.
- Cancelled opening beneath (115), Soit: \unsure{Soit}. The low internal letters merge with the strike.

### Unresolved places

- Label 119, underwritten final digit: \deleted{\unsure{8}}. Two loops remain under the live descending-tailed 9, but overlapping strokes obscure the original contour.
- Label 120, underwritten final two digits: \deleted{\unsure{19}}. The vertical beneath 2 and the loop with descending tail beneath 0 support 19, but their full original boundaries are not separable.
- Diagram 120, upper horizontal endpoint: \unsure{\rightharpoondown|{-}}. Only one short downward return is visible; a full two-sided head cannot be asserted.
- Diagram 120, cancelled suffix of lower-left object: \deleted{\unsure{\Gamma}^!}. The exclamation upright and dot are distinct; oblique hatching masks part of the Gamma body.
- Last prose line, crossed word before la: \deleted{\unsure{que}}. The first loop/return and final e-like loop survive; the crossing prevents every letter being plain.
- Last prose line, sign before son tour: \unsure{:|à}. Two separate vertically aligned marks favor a colon, but the lower body is too compressed to exclude an accented a.
- Cancelled word replaced by générisation: \unsure{spécialisation}. The s/p opening and tall interior strokes support spécialisation, but several low letters are crossed and cannot all be certified.

### Unresolved places

- (121), small cancelled mark after indexed Gamma: \unsure{\deleted{\prime}|{}_{h}}. The upright/return may be a crossed prime or a live h-like mark; neither the glyph nor its state is fully settled.
- Cancelled word before plus généralement: que. The q-shaped bowl and long descender are clear, but the following u/e strokes meet the strike; not every letter is fully exposed.
- Inserted reference after compliquée, final digit: 5. The digit has an angular upper cap but a closed lower loop. It is not fixed from the alleged earlier reference. The clear 1 and 0 and both parentheses remain outside the local doubt.
- Inserted groupoïdes at the right page edge: groupoïdes. The body of the word is visible, but its last strokes reach the right edge of the scanned paper; the whole inserted word is retained as a candidate.
- Cancelled glyph before the live category g: \mathcal G. Heavy small hatching obscures the complete original outline; C's local uncertainty is retained.
- Marginal noun after de: complexes. The p descender, l and x-like crossing favor complexes, but overlapping body notation prevents a complete secure reading.
- Marginal géométriques ending: géométriques. The beginning is readable; the ending meets the parentheses and struck glyph from the main paragraph, so its full lettering is not certified.
- Margin terminal dots: two visible dots. Two isolated points are clear; a third cannot be separated from the nearby g.
- Terminal period of (122): period. The mark is dark, separate and on the paper to the right of the target, but close enough to the page edge to merit blind confirmation.
- Arrowhead at the end of the category underline: right-pointing head. Two converging terminal strokes are visible. The draft reproduces their pointed form without inferring an additional mathematical map.
- Opening continuation, first word: \unsure{se|de}. The short initial loop/upright is compatible with both letter forms; grammar is not used to suppress the alternative.
- (121), cancelled lower annotation final index: \unsure{G'}. The final G’s lower return meets hatching; its complete outline cannot be followed.
- Adverb after plus, ending: \unsure{général\textsuperscript{t}|généralement}. A raised-t abbreviation is favored by the compact terminal mark, but the ending remains uncertain.

### Unresolved places

- Condition (123), cancelled characterization: \deleted{\unsure{isomorphe} à un \unsure{simplexe}}. The p/h outline supports isomorphe, but low internal letters and the terminal simplex candidate are obscured by the strike.
- Quotients paragraph, old adjective: \deleted{\unsure{minimaux|maximaux}}. The old line is heavily crossed. Its initial and final traces support minimaux, but the internal sequence is not wholly visible. This added uncertainty is not counted as a textual error by C.
- NB note, cancelled types: \deleted{\unsure{topologiques ou étales|topologiques ordinaires}}. The heavy strike hides low letters and the separation of ou étales versus ordinaires.
- Diagram (124), right endpoint: \overset{\varphi_G}{\unsure{\rightharpoondown|{-}}}. One short downward terminal stroke is visible, but it may be a harpoon barb or simply a flick at the end of a shaft. A conventional two-stroke arrowhead is not visible.
- Quotients paragraph, live replacement adjective: \unsure{maximaux|minimaux}. The angled medial join favors x, but compressed dots and strokes permit i/n. The quotient-order interpretation favors maximal proper quotients without settling every stroke.
- Quotient definition, right-edge punctuation: \unsure{.}. The possible point is contiguous with the black scan border in the supplementary right-edge crop.
- NB margin, trailing punctuation after tel: .\unsure{-|..}. A separate point and an elongated horizontal mark remain; the latter cannot be counted as distinct points with certainty.

### Unresolved places

- Cancelled capital at the opening: \deleted{$\unsure{M}_G$}. The crossing strokes obscure part of the two-peak capital; the subscript G is legible.
- First parenthetical connector: G\unsure{\rightharpoondown|{-}}G'. A downward end flick is visible, but its status as a deliberate small hook rather than a terminal pen movement is not certain.
- Second parenthetical connector: G\unsure{\rightharpoondown|{-}}G/C. One small downward return is drawn at the right end, without a conventional arrowhead.
- Old subscript in lower-left object of (125): \deleted{\unsure{A}}. The live C and crossing strokes cover portions of the angular earlier character.
- Curved trace at lower-right Π of (125): \deleted{\unsure{S}}\Pi. The curve crosses the first upright and continues rightward, suggesting S, but it is not sufficiently complete to settle either the old letter or its correction status.
- Second connector in the composite chain: \overset{\varphi_{G'}}{\unsure{\rightharpoondown|{-}}}. The tiny downward endpoint may be a one-sided head or a pen flick; the label φ_G′ is clear.
- Old subscript in the running factorisation sentence: \deleted{\unsure{A}}. The rounded replacement C and dark crossing strokes obstruct part of the old angular letter.

### Unresolved places

- Subscript in cancelled interlinear φ_G et: \varphi_{\unsure{G}}. Phi is clear, but the subscript overlaps diagonal cancellation strokes. G remains a candidate. The following et now has its own separate local doubt.
- Connective left of second map: \unsure{etc|et}. A c-like tail follows t but may be its flourish. There is no separate full stop. The existing second_map crop contains the complete word.
- Second-map source index: \unsure{I|\overline I}. The horizontal and upright strokes are overdrawn; the extra horizontal may be a bar or part of rewriting I.
- Body occurrence of normalisation at the right edge: normalis\unsure{at}. Only the uncertain at-like ending reaches the source boundary. No following ion is available; it is omitted, not reconstructed.
- I and closing delimiter of cancelled stable-curve pair: (\widehat X,\unsure{I)}. The opening parenthesis, hat, X and comma remain distinguishable. I and the closing delimiter overlap thick cancellation and insertion strokes.
- Base-field letter: \unsure{h|k}. The tall stem and right shoulder look h-shaped; a clear k arm cannot be isolated. Mathematical familiarity with k does not settle this lone occurrence.
- Cancelled interlinear word after phi-subscript: \unsure{et}. The tall crossed t survives; the preceding e-like loop coincides with the diagonal cancellation.

### Unresolved places

- Normalization sentence, small superscript after first A: A^{\unsure{a|e|2}}. The extra raised ink is definite, but the small double-loop/tail form is not sufficiently distinct to remove all doubt about a. Plain A is insufficient.
- Cancelled node adjective under doubles: \deleted{\unsure{quadratiques|quadruples}}. The q descender and later tall strokes support the suggested word, but the strike and right-edge compression prevent every letter being read plainly.
- Cancelled letter after lisses: \deleted{$\unsure{A}$}. The triangular body is heavily overwritten; C's doubt cannot be removed from the available ink.
- Cancelled capital after J equals A minus A-prime: \deleted{\unsure{E|En}}. E is supported by its upright and short horizontals; the rightward cancellation hides any following small n-like strokes.
- Cancelled relative after propre Y: \deleted{\unsure{qui}}. The initial loop and descent match live qui below, but the crossing obscures the following low u/i bodies.

### Unresolved places

- Cancelled prefix before the first m-graphe: \deleted{\unsure{MD}}. Neither capital is fully exposed beneath the crossing strokes.
- Stop after the first live m: m\unsure{.}graphe. The dark terminal point cannot be distinguished from the end of the low m stroke.
- Cancelled delimiter after A-tilde-prime: \deleted{\unsure{)}}. Several strokes cross the curved upright. No live closing parenthesis is supplied after it.
- Stop after the second live m: m\unsure{.}graphe. As at the first occurrence, a dark endpoint does not securely distinguish a separate stop.
- Contracted prefix in en prenant le ... graphe: \unsure{ss|s|sous|x}-graphe. The compact prefix has crossed/returned low strokes; neither the number of s bodies nor an x-like crossing is settled, and full o/u bodies are not exposed.
- First letter of the cancelled word before par: \deleted{\unsure{s}i}. The s candidate is partly hidden by the horizontal strike; the i upright and dot are visible.
- Article before the cancelled first marked-graph prefix: \unsure{un|une}. The detached small terminal body after the low un sequence could be an additional e.
- Short word before comme in the marking-set clause: \unsure{avec|on}. The very compact letter bodies do not expose each letter independently; no p descender supports prenant.

### Unresolved places

- First cancelled phrase, choisissant and partie: \deleted{\unsure{choisissant} une \unsure{partie}}. The strike hides low letters of choisissant as well as the proposed terminal partie; une remains separable.
- Second cancelled phrase, composantes and irréductibles: \deleted{de \unsure{composantes} \unsure{irréductibles}}. The long strike hides low letters in both nouns/adjectives despite the exposed descenders and ascenders.
- Cancelled old object before tilde J: \deleted{\unsure{\widetilde A}}. The triangular A body is visible, but its upper accent is mixed with the cancellation; the suggested tilde is not fully separable.
- Inserted donc formula, right-hand A accent: \widetilde J=\unsure{\widetilde A|A}. The possible tilde is entangled with the g descender of graphe on the line above. The A body is clear, but a separate accent cannot be confidently isolated.
- Opening, noun ending: construction\unsure{s}. The terminal small stroke is compressed against the preceding n-like line; ces does not force a written s.
- Inline origin-map endpoint: \unsure{\rightharpoonup|{-}}. The shaft has only an upward return and lacks the second arm present in the lower arrows.
- Cancelled application origine: \deleted{\unsure{application origine}}. Descenders support the words but the strong horizontal strike hides internal low letter bodies.

### Unresolved places

- First structure formula, S-to-N connector: \overset{\underline g}{\unsure{-|\longrightarrow}}. The terminal thickening does not separate into a secure complete arrowhead; the whole formula is cropped.
- Cancelled raised prefix: \deleted{\inserted{\unsure{pré}}}. Dense cancellation prevents certifying every letter of the raised pré candidate.
- Cancelled group before the pair: \deleted{$\unsure{f_G}$ \unsure{de}}. The mathematical-looking f_G and separate d/e-like strokes survive only partly under the strike.
- Condition a, connector: \unsure{\rightharpoondown|\longrightarrow}. The visible terminal downturn may be a one-sided head or an abbreviated ordinary arrow.
- Upper marginal genus formula, prime count: \underline g^{\unsure{\prime\prime|\prime}}. A short upper-right stroke and adjacent oblique/return may be two primes, retracing, or partly the opening parenthesis; nearby s-prime strokes do not settle their separation.
- Cancelled G argument after tilde A: \deleted{(\unsure{G})}. The outer parentheses are readable but the central G is crossed heavily.
- G index in the tilde J formula: f_{\unsure{G'|G}}. A distinct raised short stroke is present, but its prime/retrace status is unresolved.
- Cancelled argument after tilde A′: \deleted{\unsure{(G'}}. Hatching obscures the old argument, including whether any old closing delimiter survives; no hidden character is supplied.
- Diagram target A: \unsure{A|\widetilde A}. The apparent tilde coincides with strokes descending from commutatif above.
- Diagram lower-arrow glyph: \unsure{\forall}. The forall-like shape has overlapping strokes; its identity and any cancellation independent of the shaft remain unproved.
- Cancelled final On: \deleted{\unsure{On}}. The large initial curve is readable, but the black horizontal strike obscures the continuation.
- Second marked-graph abbreviation, stop: m\unsure{.}graphes. Unlike the opening occurrence, a separate dot after m is not independently exposed.
- Before the definition of tilde J: \unsure{avec|où}. Very compressed low strokes do not expose each letter or a decisive accent.
- Pale lower-left foliation, final digit: 9\unsure{4|5}. The angular middle supports 4 but faint retracing and the bowed lower part leave 5 possible.

### Unresolved places

- Single word before utiliser: \unsure{a|à|va}. One compact group; a short entry can belong to v and the small upper stroke can be an accent, without certifying either.
- 4-like glyph before couples: \unsure{\deleted{4}|4|\deleted{q}|q}. Identity and cancellation state are uncertain because the transverse stroke is compact and may belong to the glyph.
- Cancelled et before b): \deleted{\unsure{et|a}}. The small crossed loop and tall stroke do not securely separate one letter from two.
- Cancelled character after b): \deleted{\unsure{S}}. Crossing strokes obscure parts of the opposed curves.
- Cancelled word before d-prime: \deleted{\unsure{adhérence} d'}. The long strike covers much of the word. The following d and apostrophe belong to the same cancellation and are legible.
- Connector from S to component symbol: \unsure{\rightharpoondown|{-}}. Short downturned endpoint, without a secure ordinary arrowhead.
- Case of component symbol: \unsure{\Pi|\pi}_0(X). Near-cap-height bar and two downstrokes favour Pi; bowed right foot leaves pi possible. Index 0 is clear.
- Second tuple member: \unsure{\widehat A|\widehat I}. Heavy bars and central retracing do not certify letter, extra accent or successive correction states.
- Sigma index in tuple: \sigma_{\unsure{\widehat A|\widehat I}}. The same barred form recurs at small scale with retraced upper strokes.
- Cancelled formula after où: \deleted{$\unsure{\widetilde A=\sigma_{\widetilde A}|\widetilde A,\sigma_A|\widetilde A,o_A}$}. The relation and upper later body are hidden by long dark retracing; only decorated initial A and later low A are separately exposed.
- Set name after live S: \unsure{\widehat A|\widehat I}. The central letter is darkly retraced; apparent older I bars or an extra tilde are not independently established.
- Later sigma index: \sigma_{\unsure{\widetilde A|\widehat A}}. The upper stroke looks wavy but overlaps the nearby sigma stroke, leaving a hat possible.
- Final connector to S: \unsure{\rightharpoondown|{-}}. Small downturned terminal stroke, without secure full arrowhead.
- Generalisation verb at right image boundary: généralis\unsure{e}. The final small return abuts the dark right boundary and is not wholly exposed.
- Preposition before voisinage: \unsure{au|en}. The compact low letter pair does not expose the interior of its first body.
- Initial word before revient: \unsure{Il|Ça}. The connected ornate strokes include a top entry, crossing and low loop that do not securely separate the two readings.

### Unresolved places

- Opening involution subscript, M1127: \sigma_{\unsure{\widehat I|\widehat A}}. The serif crossbars favour I, but dark internal retracing includes diagonals that prevent exclusion of A. H gives definite hatted A; the final preserves both alternatives, so this uncertainty alone is not counted as a substantive H error. No distinct cancelled underlying glyph can be recovered.
- Cancelled quoted word before pts-bord: \deleted{``\unsure{bouts}''}. The zigzag strike covers parts of the middle and ending. The readable remains support bouts tentatively, but not every letter is exposed.
- Complement operand, M1130: \widetilde A=\widehat I\setminus\unsure{\widehat I^\sigma|I}. The base serif I is clear. Crowded raised ink may contain both the hat and sigma, but their components cannot be separated independently. The expected set complement has not been used to supply them.
- Cancelled word after d’un: \deleted{\unsure{couple}}. The horizontal cancellation hides the low middle letters. This is an added local uncertainty about C’s couple, not a substantive correction against C.
- Cancelled short word after I: \deleted{\unsure{se|de}}. The low heavy strike hides the bottom of the initial curved body and part of the following loop.

### Unresolved places

- First cancelled raised mark after G: \deleted{\unsure{'}}. Dense cancellation obscures the original short stroke; a prime is compatible but not certified.
- Cancelled noun after C de: \deleted{\unsure{flèches|pieds}}. The long f/l-like strokes and overall outline favour flèches, but the strike overlaps medial and terminal letters. Not every letter is plain, so the C doubt is retained.
- Second cancelled raised mark after G: \deleted{\unsure{'}}. A cancelled upper-right group is clear, but its underlying prime cannot be separated confidently from overtracing.
- M1137 after connexe: \unsure{!|\}}. The tall curling sign could be an expressive exclamation or a brace; no separate bottom dot unequivocally decides it.
- Cancelled small glyph after Si de plus on: \deleted{\unsure{f}}. A long descending stroke and crossed upper portion survive, but cancellation prevents a secure letter identification.
- Old word at the end of the last paragraph: \deleted{\unsure{préservant})}. The initial descender and short strokes are compatible with préservant; the long strike hides later letters. The closing parenthesis itself is clear and certainly crossed.

### Unresolved places

- Second normalisation, in Le composé de morphismes de … strict: \unsure{normalisation}. The prefix is clear, but the ending runs into the right edge and its individual terminal letters are not exposed. The whole-word unsure macro is compact notation for uncertainty confined to that ending, not doubt about every letter of the word. This adds uncertainty to the shared C/H word without counting a substantive correction.
- Cancelled prefix before morphe: \deleted{\unsure{iso}}. Low curved remains and a tall initial stroke are visible, but the cancellation hides enough of the letters that iso cannot be fully resolved. The replacement anti is independently legible above it.
- Inner graph letter at the right edge of the final formula: C\subset A(\unsure{G|C}). The capital is squeezed against the right edge. Its curved body is visible, but a separate G bar/tail cannot be distinguished securely from the C-like outline. Both witnesses have G; that agreement is not used to remove the local doubt. The final closing parenthesis is independently visible.

### Unresolved places

- Stop after m in the short graph term, prose baseline 7: m\unsure{.}graphes. The small terminal spot is not securely separate from the end of m. The low m itself and following graphes are clear. The added local doubt is a certainty refinement, not a substantive error against C.
- Cancelled raised mark after target G in (130): \deleted{\unsure{'}}. Repeated dark crossing strokes obscure the original short raised character. Its presence and cancelled state are clear; a prime is only the local candidate retained from C.
- Right endpoint of S-prime to S connector in the stability parenthesis: S'\unsure{-|\rightharpoondown}S. The short downturned terminal may be the end of a plain pen stroke or a downward half-head. The refinement of the arrow alternative is not a settled textual error against either witness.
- Cancelled short form before Si X: \deleted{\unsure{Supp.}}. The heavy strike crosses the connecting strokes; S and two descending p-like forms support the short candidate but do not make every letter separately certain. The full word Supposons is not visibly present.
- Graph letter before épinglée, prose baseline 18: \unsure{C|G}'. The letter is a very open C-like curve with a nearly horizontal lower end. A slight terminal thickening could be a reduced G foot, but it is not as separately traceable as the feet in clear G occurrences on this leaf. Both witness candidates remain admitted; no settled symbol correction is counted.
- Final digit of the bottom formula number: (13\unsure{0|1}). An upper tick and thick upright coexist with a rounded right loop. The first two digits 13 are clear. Neither the final value nor an old/new writing sequence is established, and sequential numbering is not used to force 131.

### Unresolved places

- Cancelled phrase in proposition b): \deleted{\unsure{composantes connexes|graphes connexes}}. A long horizontal strike hides most low letters of the first word. - Cancelled article in the corollary: \deleted{\unsure{le}}. The l-like loop is visible, but the small e-like ending lies under the strike. The cancellation state is clearer than the full underlying word.
- Small cancelled mark after G in the corollary: \deleted{\unsure{f}}. The short sloping cancellation crosses a tiny upright/curved mark. Its identity is not secure; it is kept separate from the retraced but live p of pour.

### Unresolved places

- Right end of opening prose baseline 1: \unsure{transf|transforme}. The letters reach transf and the f has a long descender and outgoing tail. Separate orme letters are not legible after it. A short form is possible, but the full form remains an alternative. No stop is supplied.
- First cancelled isomorphismes, opening baseline 2: \deleted{\unsure{isomorphismes}}. The long strike obscures connecting strokes, and descenders of the inserted équivalences cross the same area. The complete word remains a candidate, not a letter-by-letter certainty.
- Cancelled prefix before first groupoïdes, opening baseline 3: \deleted{\unsure{multi}}. Tall l/t-like strokes survive while the initial low connecting strokes and final small letter are crossed. C supplies the multi candidate and H has a struck illegible marker. The candidate is retained without counting an unresolved reading as an error against H.
- First subscript letter of the right-hand term in (144): \unsure{g|G}_\alpha. Dense retracing covers the upper loop, and a long lower loop remains. These strokes do not establish which state is final or support a recoverable cancelled-letter/replacement sequence. The uncertainty is confined to the first letter.
- Cancelled raised group after the closing index parenthesis in (144): ^{\deleted{\unsure{*}}}. Multiple crossing strokes obscure the underlying raised character. Its presence, raised placement and cancellation are clear; the star is only the tentative candidate already in C. H explicitly acknowledges the cancelled exponent in an editorial note, so no omission error is counted against H.
- Adverb after redonne at the right edge of baseline 9: \unsure{automatiq\textsuperscript{t}|automatiquement}. Auto-matiq is traceable, including the q descender. A small t-like ending at the edge appears raised, but the compressed or clipped tail does not settle the short versus full form. Both alternatives exclude H's tautologiquement.
- Second cancelled isomorphismes on baseline 10: \deleted{\unsure{isomorphismes}}. The horizontal cancellation runs through the low letter sequence, with the inserted word's descenders crossing it. The candidate remains local and doubtful rather than silently certifying the hidden letters.
- Connector between the two Pi-M objects on baseline 10: \Pi M_{G'}\unsure{-|\rightharpoondown}\Pi M_G. The single shaft has only a small downturned right end. A complete ordinary arrowhead is not secure. Both full objects, their G/G-prime indices and the absence of a Pi index 1 here are readable.
- Connector at the opening of the final paragraph: G'\unsure{-|\rightharpoondown}G. The tiny downturned terminal may be an ordinary pen ending or a downward half-head. No full conventional head is forced from the surrounding mathematical claim.
- Final adjective after morph. de norm., baseline 14: strict\unsure{s}. A miniature terminal group follows the final t, but its independence as s remains unresolved. Earlier clear plural occurrences on the leaf do not settle this separate occurrence.

### Unresolved places

- Opening parenthesis before pas: \unsure{(}pas.. The initial curved stroke is not fully separable from p. The complete closing parenthesis after strict is clear, but grammar does not certify the opener.
- Old upper-left index in146: \deleted{\unsure{G'}}. Overtracing crosses the old group; the underlying prime cannot be certified. The replacement G below it is clear.
- Top146 connector's lower-left return: \draw (topstart) -- ++(.20,-.04);
\node[below=1pt,inner sep=0pt] at (topstart) {$\unsure{\vphantom{-}}$};. The short return stroke is definite, but there is no upper-left wing. Its interpretation remains local doubt; the drawing preserves its geometry.
- Cancelled group after the right-hand product: \deleted{\unsure{1}}. The old small descending stroke is crossed densely. A1 is compatible, but not every underlying stroke is plain.
- Far-right subscript on hatted I-prime in146: \widehat I'_{\unsure{\alpha'|\alpha}}. The alpha-like body reaches the physical right edge, and any following prime is clipped or unverified. The source scan provides no further ink outside the edge.
- Lower product's cancelled group and its attachment: \prod_{\alpha'\in S'}\raisebox{-0.7ex}{\scriptsize$\deleted{\unsure{*}}$}M_{g_\alpha,\widehat I_\alpha}. The dense cancellation lies above/right of S-prime near the product sign. Its identity and attachment cannot be fixed from the ink; proximity to another cancellation does not establish common identity or history.
- Cancelled raised mark on the target g in147: g_\alpha^{\deleted{\unsure{'}}}. The raised group is clearly cancelled but its original prime is obscured.

### Unresolved places

- Opening prefix before ponctuels: \unsure{mono|uni}ponctuels. The compact low initial run does not separate the two o bodies of mono; the raised tick before p can instead be an i dot. Only the prefix is uncertain. This is added uncertainty against C, not a proved correction.
- Cancelled word within opening insertion: \deleted{\unsure{l'interaction}}. The old word is crossed through within the small raised insertion; several middle letter bodies cannot be independently recovered.
- Cancelled covariances: \deleted{\unsure{covariances}}. The dense horizontal stroke conceals much of the lower letter sequence; the provisional c/o/v beginning does not warrant removing C's doubt.
- M1154: word after dash in diagonal margin: \unsure{définies|définis}. The d/é/f beginning is visible but the small ending at the right edge of the first crop is not sufficiently separated. The blind box includes a wider region; no grammatical agreement is used to decide the e.
- Cancelled donné before G→G′: \deleted{\unsure{donné}}. The initial d and final acute survive, but the strike conceals intervening bodies. Added uncertainty does not itself make C a substantive error.
- Cancelled définir under replacement: \deleted{\unsure{définir}}. The initial défi is visible; terminal bodies are crowded by the cancellation and raised replacement. The whole-word doubt macro flags this local ending weakness, not uncertainty about every letter.
- Final displayed arrow endpoint: S\Pi_1M_G\unsure{\longrightarrow|\rightharpoondown}S\Pi_1M_{G'}.. The horizontal shaft ends in a dense raised knot with a short downward spur; two independent branches of an ordinary arrowhead cannot be confidently separated. The objects, indices, prime and full stop are clear. This adds uncertainty to the shared witness arrow rather than proving a different symbol.

### Unresolved places

- Adjective after the first l’hyp.: analytique\unsure{s}. The final low group is too compressed to separate a final s from the terminal stroke of e. No grammatical agreement is used to settle it.
- Ending of the double-underlined adjective: attaché\unsure{s|es}. The ending after attaché contains compressed low strokes. A separate additional e is not established.
- Opening delimiter/equality order in the Delta insertion: \Delta_x\unsure{=(|(=}\Delta_x. The curved opening stroke overlaps the equality and the left edge of the second Delta. The horizontal placement does not securely establish the intended order.
- Final argument of the Delta insertion: X\unsure{,D}. X is visible, but the cramped comma and possible D at the right edge cannot be separated confidently. Both closing curves are retained.
- Small mark before the power-set P: \unsure{à|:}. Two tiny separated strokes can be an abbreviated a with accent or a colon. The lower stroke does not establish a clear loop.
- Cancelled continuation after Si: \deleted{Si \unsure{$J$ est}}. Si is traceable, but the long strike and strokes of the raised replacement obstruct the J/est sequence. The local candidate is retained without certifying each covered letter.
- Cancelled word before de D_{x,J}: \deleted{\unsure{une}}. The strike crosses nearly all low connecting strokes; une remains a candidate, not a settled complete reading.

### Unresolved places

- 150: extra glyph: \unsure{\xi}. The extra ink cannot be omitted, but its letter identity and possible previous state are not recoverable from the crossing strokes.
- 151: first function letter: \unsure{d|c}(x)<c(y). Overlapping c-like curves and a d-like upright prevent a firm reading. The second c and both written less-than signs are clear.
- Upper cancelled dire: \unsure{dire}. The stroke conceals internal bodies; the short exposed run favours dire, without allowing an unqualified restoration.
- Upper cancelled ens.: d'\unsure{ens.}. The d/apostrophe and final point survive; the low middle bodies are crossed.
- Upper cancelled ordonnés: \unsure{ordonnés}. The cancellation and interlinear writing crowd the word, especially its ending; H’s ending and following comma remain an open collation.
- Cancelled retraced a after J: \unsure{a}. The short word has more than one dense loop/trace; no additional letters or rewriting chronology are invented.
- Cancelled ce before qui: \unsure{ce}. The small curves immediately after the parenthesis are weak; ce is retained provisionally without claiming H’s omission proved.
- Cancelled adjective: \unsure{maximale|minimale}. The adjective meets a diagonal stroke and the far-right edge; the distinction remains unresolved and is not decided by the mathematics.
- Nested cancelled qui est: \deleted{\unsure{qui est}}. The separate horizontal strike leaves the q descender and some short bodies visible but conceals the word sequence in part.
- Raised parameter: \unsure{q|d}\in\mathbb N^*. The retraced head has a right-loop p-like appearance as well as the low tail. Comparison with the two later forms does not recover a single certain letter.
- Parameter after codim: \unsure{q|d}. A high d-like stem and a lower q-like tail both remain; no sequence of correction is established.
- Old cancelled voit: \deleted{\unsure{voit}}. The heavy horizontal strike conceals the initial bodies; the live following voit is clear. The uncertainty concerns only the older cancelled token.
- D subscript at the foot: D_{\unsure{q|d}}. The tiny subscript is heavily retraced, with both a high stem and curled lower tail. The parameter’s identity is still open.
